, it implies that
s_{1}=\mathrm{nil}
and
s_{2}=\mathrm{nil}
,
and therefore the relations above are
\Gamma_{0};\mathrm{nil}\rightarrowtail\Gamma_{1};\mathrm{nil}
and
\Gamma_{0};\mathrm{nil}\rightarrowtail\Gamma_{2};\mathrm{nil}
.

By induction hypothesis on
\Gamma_{0};\mathrm{nil}\vdash t_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t_{1}
, there exists a term
t_{3}
such that:

•

\Gamma_{1};\mathrm{nil}\vdash t_{1}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t_{3}

•

\Gamma_{2};\mathrm{nil}\vdash t_{2}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t_{3}

By rule
Ct-Ann
, we know:

•

\Gamma_{0},x\,{\leq}\,t_{0};\mathrm{nil}\rightarrowtail\Gamma_{1},x\,{\leq}\,t_{1};\mathrm{nil}
from
\Gamma_{0};\mathrm{nil}\vdash t_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t_{1}

•

\Gamma_{0},x\,{\leq}\,t_{0};\mathrm{nil}\rightarrowtail\Gamma_{2},x\,{\leq}\,t_{2};\mathrm{nil}
from
\Gamma_{0};\mathrm{nil}\vdash t_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t_{2}

By induction hypothesis on
\Gamma_{0},x\,{\leq}\,t_{0};\mathrm{nil}\vdash u_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{1}
, there exists a term
u_{3}
such that:

•

\Gamma_{1},x\,{\leq}\,t_{1};\mathrm{nil}\vdash u_{1}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{3}

•

\Gamma_{2},x\,{\leq}\,t_{2};\mathrm{nil}\vdash u_{2}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{3}

We now have:

•

\Gamma_{1};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{1}.u_{1}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{3}.u_{3}

by rule
Me-Fun

from
\Gamma_{1};\mathrm{nil}\vdash t_{1}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t_{3}
and
\Gamma_{1},x\,{\leq}\,t_{1};\mathrm{nil}\vdash u_{1}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{3}

•

\Gamma_{2};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{2}.u_{2}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{3}.u_{3}

by rule
Me-Fun

from
\Gamma_{2};\mathrm{nil}\vdash t_{2}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t_{3}
and
\Gamma_{2},x\,{\leq}\,t_{2};\mathrm{nil}\vdash u_{2}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{3}

The filled diagram is therefore the following:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{2}.u_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{3}.u_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{0}.u_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{1}.u_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}

Case
Me-FOp
with
Me-FOp
:

We need to prove the diagram below:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{2}.u_{2}}
.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{0}.u_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{1}.u_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}

By assumption, we have the following reductions:

•

\Gamma_{0};\alpha_{0}::s_{0}\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{0}.u_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{1}.u_{1}
by rule
Me-FOp
, which gives us:

–

\Gamma_{0};\mathrm{nil}\vdash t_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t_{1}

–

\Gamma_{0},x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha_{0};s_{0}\vdash u_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{1}

•

\Gamma_{0};\alpha_{0}::s_{0}\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{0}.u_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{2}.u_{2}
by rule
Me-FOp
, which gives us:

–

\Gamma_{0};\mathrm{nil}\vdash t_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t_{2}

–

\Gamma_{0},x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha_{0};s_{0}\vdash u_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{2}

•

The stack of our case is
s=\alpha_{0}::s_{0}

Let
\Gamma_{1};\alpha_{1}::s_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2};\alpha_{2}::s_{2}
be extended contexts such that

\Gamma_{0};\alpha_{0}::s_{0}\rightarrowtail\Gamma_{1};\alpha_{1}::s_{1}

and
\Gamma_{0};\alpha_{0}::s_{0}\rightarrowtail\Gamma_{2};\alpha_{2}::s_{2}
.
By rule
Ct-Stk
, those relations respectively implies

\Gamma_{0};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\alpha_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha_{1}
and
\Gamma_{0};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\alpha_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha_{2}
.

By Lemma
36
, respectively
\Gamma_{0};\alpha_{0}::s_{0}\rightarrowtail\Gamma_{1};\alpha_{1}::s_{1}

and
\Gamma_{0};\alpha_{0}::s_{0}\rightarrowtail\Gamma_{2};\alpha_{2}::s_{2}

implies respectively

\Gamma_{0};\mathrm{nil}\rightarrowtail\Gamma_{1};\mathrm{nil}
and
\Gamma_{0};\mathrm{nil}\rightarrowtail\Gamma_{2};\mathrm{nil}
.
By induction hypothesis on
\Gamma_{0};\mathrm{nil}\vdash t_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t_{1}
, there exists a term
t_{3}
such that:

•

\Gamma_{1};\mathrm{nil}\vdash t_{1}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t_{3}

•

\Gamma_{2};\mathrm{nil}\vdash t_{2}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t_{3}

By rule
Ct-Ann
, we know:

•

\Gamma_{0},x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha_{0};s_{0}\rightarrowtail\Gamma_{1},x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha_{1};s_{1}
from
\Gamma_{0};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\alpha_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha_{1}

•

\Gamma_{0},x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha_{0};s_{0}\rightarrowtail\Gamma_{2},x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha_{2};s_{2}
from
\Gamma_{0};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\alpha_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha_{2}

By induction hypothesis on
Gamma_{0},x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha_{0};s_{0}\vdash u_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{1}
, there exists a term
u_{3}
such that:

•

\Gamma_{1},x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha_{1};s_{1}\vdash u_{1}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{3}

•

\Gamma_{2},x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha_{2};s_{2}\vdash u_{2}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{3}

We now have:

•

\Gamma_{1};\alpha_{1}::s_{1}\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{1}.u_{1}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{3}.u_{3}
by rule
Me-FOp

from
\Gamma_{1};\mathrm{nil}\vdash t_{1}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t_{3}
and
\Gamma_{1},x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha_{1};s_{1}\vdash u_{1}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{3}

•

\Gamma_{2};\alpha_{2}::s_{2}\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{2}.u_{2}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{3}.u_{3}
by rule
Me-FOp

from
\Gamma_{2};\mathrm{nil}\vdash t_{2}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t_{3}
and
\Gamma_{2},x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha_{2};s_{2}\vdash u_{2}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{3}

The filled diagram is therefore the following:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{2}.u_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{3}.u_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{0}.u_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{1}.u_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}

Case
Me-Bet
with
Me-Bet
:

We need to prove the diagram below:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{2}[x\backslash v_{2}]}
.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u_{0})v_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{1}[x\backslash v_{1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}

By assumption, we have the following reductions:

•

\Gamma_{0};s_{0}\vdash(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u_{0})v_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{1}[x\backslash v_{1}]
by rule

, which gives us:

–

\Gamma_{0};s_{0}\vdash u_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{1}

–

\Gamma_{0};\mathrm{nil}\vdash v_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v_{1}

•

\Gamma_{0};s_{0}\vdash(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u_{0})v_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{2}[x\backslash v_{2}]

, which gives us:

–

\Gamma_{0};s_{0}\vdash u_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{2}

\Gamma_{0};\mathrm{nil}\vdash v_{0}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v_{2}

•

\Gamma_{0};s_{0}\rightarrowtail\Gamma_{1};s_{1}

•

\Gamma_{0};s_{0}\rightarrowtail\Gamma_{2};s_{2}

Let
\Gamma_{1};s_{1}
and

and

.

) we have:

and
\Gamma_{2};\mathrm{nil}\vdash v_{2}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v_{3}

The filled diagram is therefore the following:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{2}[x\backslash v_{2}]}

:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Top}}

Theorem
3
(Transivity is admissible).

Let
\Gamma;s
be an extended context.
Let
u
and
v
be terms.
If
\Gamma;s\vdash u\leq^{*}v
then
\Gamma;s\vdash u\leq v
.

Proof.

By induction on the number of intermediary steps of the derivation
\Gamma;s\vdash u\leq^{*}v
.
The base, which is either 0 or 1 derivation yields the result
\Gamma;s\vdash u\leq v
.
Else, it suffices to prove that for every
terms
t
,
u
, and
v
, if
\Gamma;s\vdash t\leq u
and
\Gamma;s\vdash u\leq v
, then

\Gamma;s\vdash t\leq v
. From
\Gamma;s\vdash t\leq u
and
\Gamma;s\vdash u\leq v
we
obtain the diagram below
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
.
.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\leq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\leq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\leq}

where the solid edges correspond to the definition of the subtyping relation. The result

\Gamma;s\vdash t\leq v
follows by completing the diagram with the dashed arrows, which
can be done by commutativity of
\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}
and
\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}
established in
Theorem
1
below.
∎

Theorem
4
(Progress).

Let
t
be a term. For every logical context
\Gamma
, if
\Gamma\vdash t~\mathrm{wf}
then either
t
is
in normal form, or there exists a term
t^{\prime}
such that
t\mapsto t^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By structural induction on the term
t
. If
t
is
\mathsf{Top}
or a variable
x
, then the result holds
since
t
is already in normal form. Therefore it suffices to consider the following cases:

Case
t=\lambda x\,{\leq}\,u.v
with
u\not\in\mathsf{NF}
:

By assumption we have

\Gamma\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,u.v~\mathrm{wf}
. By rule
Wf-Fun
,
we obtain
\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
. By the induction hypothesis
on
u
, there exists
u^{\prime}
such that
u\mapsto u^{\prime}
, and as a result

t\mapsto\lambda x\,{\leq}\,u^{\prime}.v
by rule
Os-Con
.

Case
t=\lambda x\,{\leq}\,u_{n}.v
with
v\not\in\mathsf{NF}
:

By assumption we have

\Gamma\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,u_{n}.v~\mathrm{wf}
. By rule
Wf-Fun
,
we have that
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,u_{n}\vdash v~\mathrm{wf}
.
By using the induction hypothesis on
v
, there exists
v^{\prime}

such that
v\mapsto v^{\prime}
, and as a result
t\mapsto\lambda x\,{\leq}\,u_{n}.v^{\prime}
by rule

Os-Con
.

Case
t=u\,v
with
u\not\in\mathsf{NF}
:

By assumption we have

\Gamma\vdash u\,v~\mathrm{wf}
. By rule
Wf-App
and Proposition
12
we have

\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
. Since
u
is not a normal form and by the induction
hypothesis, there exists a term
u^{\prime}
such that
u\mapsto u^{\prime}
, and therefore

t\mapsto u^{\prime}\,v
by rule
Os-Con
.

Case
t=u_{n}\,v
with
v\not\in\mathsf{NF}
.

By assumption we have

\Gamma\vdash u_{n}\,v~\mathrm{wf}
.
By rule
Wf-App
and Proposition
12
we have

\Gamma\vdash v~\mathrm{wf}
. Since
v
is not a normal form and by the induction
hypothesis, there exists a term
v^{\prime}
such that
v\mapsto v^{\prime}
, and therefore

t\mapsto u_{n}\,v^{\prime}
by rule
Os-Con
.

Case
t=u_{n}\,v_{n}
with
u_{n}\in\mathsf{NF}
and
v_{n}\in\mathsf{NF}
.

Term
u_{n}
is either
\mathsf{Top}
, a
function, or a neutral applied to multiple normal forms. If
u_{n}
is a function, then
t
is a redex can be reduced by
rule
Os-Bet
.
The case where
u_{n}
is
\mathsf{Top}
is impossible, indeed
from the fact that
t
is well-formed, by rule
W-App
we have
u_{n}
a subtype of a function
\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime}.\mathsf{Top}
.
If
u_{n}
is
\mathsf{Top}
, it cannot have a function supertype by Lemma
11
.
Else, if
u_{n}
is a neutral applied to multiple normal forms,
then
t=u_{n}\,v_{n}
, because
v_{n}
is in normal form, is also a neutral applied to multiple normal forms,
and therefore
t
is in normal form. ∎

Theorem
5
(Preservation).

Let
\Gamma
be a logical context.
Let
t
,
t^{\prime}
, and
u
be terms. If

\Gamma\vdash t\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}u
and
t\mapsto t^{\prime}
, then

\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}u
.

Proof.

To prove
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}u
we use rule
Ws-Trs

and prove
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}t
and
\Gamma\vdash t\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}u
.
We already have
\Gamma\vdash t\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}u
, so we only prove
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}t
.

From the assumption
\Gamma\vdash t\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}u
and by
Proposition
12
we know that

\Gamma\vdash t~\mathrm{wf}
. We obtain
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf}
from the
\Gamma\vdash t~\mathrm{wf}
established
earlier and by Lemma
6
and
t\mapsto t^{\prime}
.

By rule
Ws-Rfl
and
Ws-Sub
, we have

\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}t^{\prime}
,
hence
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}t
by rule
Ws-Rgh
.
∎

Lemma
6
(Evaluation preserves well-formedness).

Let
\Gamma
be a logical context.
Let
t
and
t^{\prime}
be terms such that
\Gamma\vdash t~\mathrm{wf}

and
t\mapsto t^{\prime}
.
We have
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf}
.

Proof.

We prove that
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf}
by induction on
t\mapsto t^{\prime}
.
We distinguish based on the last rule used in the derivation
t\mapsto t^{\prime}
.

Case
t=(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,u.v)w
and
t^{\prime}=v[x\backslash w]
by rule
Os-Bet
:

By the assumption

\Gamma\vdash(\lambda x\leq u.v)w~\mathrm{wf}

and by rule
Wf-App
, we have

\Gamma\vdash\lambda x\leq u.v\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda x\leq z.\mathsf{Top}
and

\Gamma\vdash w\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}z
.
By inversion (Lemma
10
),
we obtain
\Gamma\vdash u\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}z
.
By Lemma
15
, we obtain
\Gamma\vdash z\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}u
,
hence by Lemma
16

\Gamma\vdash z\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}u
.
Because both
z
and
u
are well-formed in
\Gamma
, we have
\Gamma\vdash z\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}u
by rule
Ws-Sub
.
By transitivity, we now have
\Gamma\vdash w\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}u
.

From
\Gamma\vdash\lambda x\leq u.v\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda x\leq z.\mathsf{Top}

and Proposition
12
,
we obtain
\Gamma\vdash\lambda x\leq u.v~\mathrm{wf}
.
Hence by rule
Wf-Fun
, we obtain
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,u\vdash v~\mathrm{wf}
.

Now, by Lemma
7
on

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,u\vdash v~\mathrm{wf}
and
\Gamma\vdash w\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}u
,
we obtain
\Gamma\vdash v[x\backslash w]~\mathrm{wf}
.

Case
t=\lambda x\,{\leq}\,u.v
and
t^{\prime}=\lambda x\,{\leq}\,u^{\prime}.v
by rule
Os-Con
:

By assumption,
\Gamma\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,u.v~\mathrm{wf}
holds, and we have
u\mapsto u^{\prime}
.

By assumption, we have
\Gamma\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,u.v~\mathrm{wf}
. By rule

Wf-Fun
we obtain
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,u\vdash v~\mathrm{wf}
and

\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
.
By using the induction hypothesis on
u\mapsto u^{\prime}
we obtain
\Gamma\vdash u^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf}
.
From Proposition
17
, we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
23
, we obtain
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,u^{\prime}\vdash v~\mathrm{wf}
. Finally, we
obtain
\Gamma\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,u^{\prime}.v~\mathrm{wf}
by application of rule

Wf-Fun
.

Case
t=\lambda x\,{\leq}\,u.v
and
t^{\prime}=\lambda x\,{\leq}\,u.v^{\prime}
by rule
Os-Con
:

By assumption,
\Gamma\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,u.v~\mathrm{wf}
holds, and we have
v\mapsto v^{\prime}
.

By rule
Wf-Fun
we have that
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,u\vdash v~\mathrm{wf}
, and by the induction
hypothesis on
v\mapsto v^{\prime}
we obtain
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,u\vdash v^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf}
since

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,u\vdash v~\mathrm{wf}
.
Finally, we obtain
\Gamma\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,u.v^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf}
by application of rule
Wf-Fun
.

Case
t=u\,v
and
t^{\prime}=u^{\prime}\,v
by rule
Os-Con
:

By assumption,

\Gamma\vdash u\,v~\mathrm{wf}
holds. By rule
Wf-App
and Proposition

12
, we obtain that
\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
.
By applying the induction hypothesis on
u\mapsto u^{\prime}
,
we obtain
\Gamma\vdash u^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf}
.
By rule
Wf-App

we obtain
\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,w.\mathsf{Top}
and
\Gamma\vdash v\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}w
.
Hence
\Gamma\vdash u^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,w.\mathsf{Top}
,
By Lemma
27

Finally, we obtain
\Gamma\vdash u^{\prime}\,v~\mathrm{wf}
by application of rules
Wf-App
from

\Gamma\vdash u^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,w.\mathsf{Top}
and
\Gamma\vdash v\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}w
.

Case
t=u\,v
and
t^{\prime}=u\,v^{\prime}
by rule
Os-Con
:

By assumption,
\Gamma\vdash u\,v~\mathrm{wf}
holds. By rule
Wf-App
,
Propositions
12
and
we obtain
\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
,
\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,w.\mathsf{Top}
,
\Gamma\vdash v~\mathrm{wf}
,

\Gamma\vdash v\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}w
and
\Gamma\vdash w~\mathrm{wf}
. By the induction hypothesis on
v\mapsto v^{\prime}
, we obtain

\Gamma\vdash v^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf}
.
By Lemma
27
, we also obtain
\Gamma\vdash v^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}w
.

The result finally

\Gamma\vdash u\,v^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf}
holds by application of rules
Wf-App

\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,w.\mathsf{Top}
, and

\Gamma\vdash v^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}w
.∎

Lemma
7
(Substitution preserves well-formedness).

Let
\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
be logical contexts, and let
t
,
u
and
\alpha
be terms.
Let
x
be a variable.
If

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
, and

\Gamma\vdash\alpha\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}t
,
then

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash u[x\backslash\alpha]~\mathrm{wf}
.

Proof.

First, we establish that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
is prevalid by
Lemma
28
, which we’ll use in most of the cases
below. Now we proceed by induction on the derivation tree of

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
. We distinguish cases on the last rule used:

Rule
Wf-PrS
or
Wf-PrE
, with
u=y
and
y\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime}\in\Gamma
:

By assumption, we have
y\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime}\in\Gamma
hence

y\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime}\in\Gamma\cup\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
As proven at the start of this lemma, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
prevalid,
hence
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash y~\mathrm{wf}
by rule
Wf-PrS
or
Wf-PrE
.

Rule
Wf-PrS
or
Wf-PrE
, with
u=y
and
y\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{\prime}
:

From the fact that
y\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime}
is a subtype annotation of
\Gamma^{\prime}
, we have that

y\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
is a subtype annotation of
\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
by
definition of the substitution of extended context which doesn’t change the domain of the logical context.
We also have
y\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\in\Gamma\cup\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]

from
y\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\in\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
As proven at the start of this lemma, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
prevalid,
we can now conclude that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash y~\mathrm{wf}
by rule
Wf-PrS
or
Wf-PrE
.

Rule
Wf-PrS
or
Wf-PrE
, with
u=x
:

By assumption of our lemma, we have
\Gamma\vdash\alpha~\mathrm{wf}
,
hence by weakening (Lemma
19
),
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash\alpha~\mathrm{wf}
,
which is the desired result of this case.

Rule
Wf-Top
, with
u=\mathsf{Top}
:

We have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash\mathsf{Top}~\mathrm{wf}
since

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
is prevalid and by rule
Wf-Top
.

Rule
Wf-Fun
, with
u=\lambda y\,{\leq}\,v.u^{\prime}
:

Without loss of generality we can assume that
y\not=x
because we can always alpha convert the variable
y
to a different name.
By assumption and by the rule used, we
have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash v~\mathrm{wf}
and

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime},y\,{\leq}\,v\vdash u^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf}
. By the induction hypothesis, we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash v[x\backslash\alpha]~\mathrm{wf}
and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha],y\,{\leq}\,v[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash u^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]~\mathrm{wf}
.
Now, we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash\lambda y\,{\leq}\,v[x\backslash\alpha].u^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]~\mathrm{wf}
by rule
Wf-Fun
,
which equals
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,v.u^{\prime})[x\backslash\alpha]~\mathrm{wf}

since
y\not=x
by definition of the substitution.

Rule
Wf-App
with
u=v\,u^{\prime}
:

By
Wf-App
on our assumption, we have

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}z
and

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash v\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.\mathsf{Top}
.

Let’s prove by induction on

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}z

that we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash u^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}z[x\backslash\alpha]
.
We distinguish cases based on the last rule used in the derivation
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}z
.

Rule
Ws-Sub
:

In this case our derivation is
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}z

with premises
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u^{\prime}\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}z
,
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf}

and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash z~\mathrm{wf}
.
In that case, by the result we will prove below, we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash u^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}z[x\backslash\alpha]
.

Rule
Ws-Trs
:

In this case our derivation is
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}z

with premises
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v
and

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash v\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.
By induction on these two derivations, we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash u^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v[x\backslash\alpha]
and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash v[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}z[x\backslash\alpha]
.
By rule
Ws-Trs
, we now have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash u^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}z[x\backslash\alpha]
.

We now prove that for any
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}b
,
subtree of
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}z
,
with both
a
and
b
well-formed in
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}
,
then we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}b[x\backslash\alpha]
.
We first prove an auxiliary result by induction, and then conclude our desired derivation

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}b[x\backslash\alpha]
.

We now prove that for any
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}b
being a
subtree of
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}z
,
then there exists three terms
a^{\prime}
,
b^{\prime}
and
c
such that

•

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}c[x\backslash\alpha]
,

•

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}c[x\backslash\alpha]
,

•

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
,

•

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
only if
a^{\prime}\neq b^{\prime}
.

It is crucial to work on a subtrees of
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}z

because we use the main induction hypothesis on well-formed derivation trees.
Here is a diagram which illustrates the above conditions:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b[x\backslash\alpha]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c[x\backslash\alpha]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a[x\backslash\alpha]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\equiv}

We prove this result by induction on
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}b
.
We distinguish based on the last rule used in the derivation of
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}b
:

Case
Ws-Rfl
:

In our case, we have
a=b
, and with
a^{\prime}=b^{\prime}=c=a
, we have the desired result
because
\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}
is reflexive.

Case
Ws-Lf1
:

In this case, we have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}b
with premises

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a_{0}\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}b
, and

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a_{0}
.
By using the subinduction, we know there exists three terms
a^{\prime}
,
b^{\prime}
and
c
such that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}c[x\backslash\alpha]
, and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}c[x\backslash\alpha]
, and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
, and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
only if
a^{\prime}\neq b^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
31
on
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a_{0}
,
we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
By transitivity of
\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}
, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
,
which concludes this case.

Case
Ws-Lf2
:

In this case, we have

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}b
with premises

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a_{0}\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}b
, and

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a_{0}
, and

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a~\mathrm{wf}
, and

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a_{0}~\mathrm{wf}
.
By applying the induction hypothesis on
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a~\mathrm{wf}
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]~\mathrm{wf}
.
By applying the induction hypothesis on
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a_{0}~\mathrm{wf}
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]~\mathrm{wf}
.
By using the subinduction, we know there exists three terms
a^{\prime}
,
b^{\prime}
and
c
such that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}c[x\backslash\alpha]
, and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}c[x\backslash\alpha]
, and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
, and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
only if
a^{\prime}\neq b^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
9
on
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a_{0}
,
we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}a_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]
.

We do a case disjunction on if
a^{\prime}=b^{\prime}
or not.

If
a^{\prime}=b^{\prime}
:

If
a^{\prime}=b^{\prime}
, then
the derivation of our assumption

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}c[x\backslash\alpha]

is actually
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}c[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Therefore we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}c[x\backslash\alpha]

by transitivity of
\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}
from

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}c[x\backslash\alpha]
.
then picking
a^{\prime}=a
,
b^{\prime}=a_{0}
,
c=c
and
b=b
, we have the desired result.
Indeed we have:

•

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}c[x\backslash\alpha]
,
which we obtained with our subinduction.

•

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}c[x\backslash\alpha]
,
which we obtained above.

•

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}a[x\backslash\alpha]
.
By reflexivity on
\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}
.

•

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}a_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]
only if
a\neq a_{0}
.
We prove above that we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}a_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]
.

Else,

Now, we will transform

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]

into
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
First, by rule
Ws-Rfl
, we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
By induction on the number of derivation of
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
,
we apply the rule
Ws-Lf1
at each step to transform

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
into

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
By Lemma
12
, we know that
both
a_{0}
and
a^{\prime}
are well-formed in context
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
from respectively

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}a_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]
and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Hence, by rule
Ws-Sub

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.

By transitivity (rule
Ws-Trs
), we know have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
,
and then by transitivity again, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.

This effectively concludes the case, because we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}a[x\backslash\alpha]
by reflexivity,

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}b[x\backslash\alpha]
, and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.

Case
Ws-Rgh
:

In this case, we have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}b
with premises

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}b_{0}
, and

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b_{0}
.
By using the subinduction, we know there exists three terms
a^{\prime}
,
b^{\prime}
and
c
such that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}c[x\backslash\alpha]
, and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}c[x\backslash\alpha]
, and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
, and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
only if
a^{\prime}\neq b^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
31
on
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b_{0}

we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Hence by transitivity of
\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}
, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}c[x\backslash\alpha]
.
This concludes this case.

Therefore, we obtained that for any sub-derivation
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}b

of
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}z
,
there exists three terms
a^{\prime}
,
b^{\prime}
and
c
such that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}c[x\backslash\alpha]
, and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}c[x\backslash\alpha]
, and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
, and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
We also know that both
a
and
b
are well-formed in
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}
.
Let’s finish by proving that we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}b[x\backslash\alpha]
.

By using the induction hypothesis, we obtain that both
a[x\backslash\alpha]
and
b[x\backslash\alpha]

are well-formed in context
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
By Proposition
12

applied to
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]

yields that both
a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
and
b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
are well-formed in context
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.

We now build

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
By rule
Ws-Rfl
, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
By induction on the number of derivation of
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
,
we apply the rule
Ws-Lf1
at each step to transform

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
into

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Because both terms are well-formed, by rule
Ws-Sub
, we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.

By a similar reasoning, and because both
b[x\backslash\alpha]
and
b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]

are well-formed in
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
we have by a similar reasoning

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}b[x\backslash\alpha]
.

Finally, from all above, by transitivity (rule
Ws-Trs
), we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}b[x\backslash\alpha]
the desired result.

Let’s now prove that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash v[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.\mathsf{Top})[x\backslash\alpha]
from

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash v\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.\mathsf{Top}
.
As above, we first proceed by induction on the number of transitivity step in
\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}
,
and prove each step by a subinduction as above which we omit it here,
because the previous reasoning do not depend on the structure of the terms
u^{\prime}
and
z
, the same
reasoning can be made on
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash v\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.\mathsf{Top}
.
So we obtain
Therefore, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash v[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.\mathsf{Top})[x\backslash\alpha]
.

Finally, we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash v[x\backslash\alpha]\,u^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]~\mathrm{wf}
by rule
Wf-App
.
∎

Lemma 9
.

Let
\Gamma;s
be an extended context, and
\Gamma^{\prime}
be a logical context,
such that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}
is prevalid.
Let
u
,
v
,
t
and
\alpha
be terms, such that
both
u[x\backslash\alpha]
and
v[x\backslash\alpha]
are well-formed in context
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.

If
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
,
and
\Gamma\vdash\alpha\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}t
.

Then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash u[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v[x\backslash\alpha]
.

Proof.

In this lemma, we have two cases, either the derivation
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v

depending on if our derivation is of the form
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\mathsf{Co}[x]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Co}[t]
for some covariant context
\mathsf{Co}
.

If it is:

By assumption, we have both
u[x\backslash\alpha]
and
v[x\backslash\alpha]
well-formed in context
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
,
because the derivation
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v

is
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\mathsf{Co}[x]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Co}[t]
,
these terms are
\mathsf{Co}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[\alpha]
and
\mathsf{Co}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[t]
.
From
\Gamma\vdash\alpha\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}t
we have by weakening (Lemma
19
)

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash\alpha\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}t
.
We therefore have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash\alpha\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}t
,
and both
\mathsf{Co}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[\alpha]
and
\mathsf{Co}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[t]
well-formed in
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Hence by Conjecture
8
, we obtain

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash\mathsf{Co}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\mathsf{Co}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[t]
.
Because there is no instance of the variable
x
in neither
\alpha
nor in
t
,
this last derivation is

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash u[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v[x\backslash\alpha]
.
By prevalidity of
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
, we know that there is no instance of the variable
x

in
\Gamma
. As a result, there is no instance of this variable anywhere.
As a result, we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash u[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v[x\backslash\alpha]
the desired result.

Else:

We apply Lemma
30
to obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash u[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v[x\backslash\alpha]
.
By rule
Ws-Rfl
, we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash v[x\backslash\alpha]\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Hence, by rule
Ws-Lft
,

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash u[x\backslash\alpha]\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Because both terms are well-formed by assumption, we have by rule
Ws-Sub
,

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash u[x\backslash\alpha]\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v[x\backslash\alpha]

the desired result.

∎

Lemma 10

(Inversion lemma.)
.

Let
\Gamma
be a logical context.
Let
\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u
and
\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime}.u^{\prime}
be terms.
If
\Gamma\vdash(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u)\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime}.u^{\prime})
then

\Gamma\vdash t\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}t^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on the number of transitivity step of

\Gamma\vdash(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u)\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime}.u^{\prime})
,
we obtain
\Gamma\vdash(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u)\leq^{*}(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime}.u^{\prime})

by applying Proposition
13
on each of these transitivity steps.
By transitivity elimination (Lemma
3
), we then obtain

\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u)\leq(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime}.u^{\prime})
.
By definition, there exists a term
z
such that

\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u)\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
and

\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime}.u^{\prime})\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
.
On the first derivation, based on the structure of the term
\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u
,
only the rule
Me-Fun
can be applied, and therefore there exists a function

\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{z}.u_{z}
such that
z=\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t_{z}.u_{z}
.
Because only the rule
Me-Fun
has been applied, we also have

\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t_{z}
.
We apply the same reasoning on the derivation
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime}.u^{\prime})\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
,
which consists only of use of the rule
Ms-Fun
or
Ms-Equ
with
Me-Fun
,
and therefore we have as well
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t_{z}
.

By rule
Ws-Rfl
, we have
\Gamma\vdash t_{z}\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}t_{z}
.
We now show that we have
\Gamma\vdash t\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}t_{z}
by induction on the number of derivation steps of

\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t_{z}
. If there is no derivation step, then we have
t=t_{z}
and the result holds.
Otherwise, there exists a term
t^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime}

and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t_{z}
. By the induction hypothesis on the number of derivation steps of

\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t_{z}
, we obtain
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}t_{z}
.
By rule
Ws-Lf1
on
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime}
and
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}t_{z}
,
we obtain
\Gamma\vdash t\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}t_{z}
.
By the same reasoning on the number of derivation steps of
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t_{z}
,
we obtain
\Gamma\vdash t\equiv t^{\prime}
from
\Gamma\vdash t\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}t_{z}
and the rule
Ws-Rgh
.
∎

Theorem 11

(No supertype of Top.)
.

Let
\Gamma;s
be an extended context.
Let
\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u
be a term. We cannot
have
\Gamma;s\vdash\mathsf{Top}\leq^{*}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u
.

Proof.

First we transform
\Gamma;s\vdash\mathsf{Top}\leq^{*}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u

into
\Gamma;s\vdash\mathsf{Top}\leq\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u
by transitivity elimination (Lemma
3
).
Assume towards a contradiction that

\Gamma;s\vdash\mathsf{Top}\leq\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u
. By definition of the subtyping relation, there exists
w

such that
\Gamma;s\vdash\mathsf{Top}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}w
and
\Gamma;s\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}w
.
From
\Gamma;s\vdash\mathsf{Top}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}w
, we obtain that
w=\mathsf{Top}

since
\mathsf{Top}
can only promote to itself, with rules
Ms-Equ
and

Ms-Top
. Now from
\Gamma;s\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}w
, we know that
w
is an
abstraction since the only rule for equivalence reduction that can be applied to

\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u
is rule
Ms-Fun
or
Ms-FOp
. But this contradicts the
w=\mathsf{Top}
established
earlier and we are done.
∎

Appendix B
Rest of the appendix

Proposition 12

(Well-formedness extraction)
.

Let
\Gamma
be an extended context and
u
and
v
be terms. If

\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v
then both derivation
\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
and

\Gamma\vdash v~\mathrm{wf}
exist in the derivation tree
\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.

Proof.

Note: the requirement that the derivation trees (
\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
and
\Gamma\vdash v~\mathrm{wf}
)
exist in the original derivation tree is used on other lemmas because we do induction on derivation trees.
It is not enough to know that
\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
and
\Gamma\vdash v~\mathrm{wf}
exist,
they need to be a subtree of our original tree. Hence this formulation.

By induction on the derivation tree of
\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v
. If the last
rule applied is
Ws-Sub
, then the result holds from the assumption of the rule.
Else, the last rule applied is
Wf-Trans
, then there exists a term
t
such that

\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}t
and
\Gamma\vdash t\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v
, and the result
holds by the induction hypothesis.
∎

Proposition 13

(From well-subtyping to subtyping.)
.

Let
\Gamma
be a logical context and
u
and
v
be terms. If

\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v
then
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\leq v
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation tree of
\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.
We distinguish cases based on the last rule used in the derivation tree.

Rule
Ws-Rfl
:

In this case, we have
u=v=t
.
By Proposition
18
, we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t
.
By rule
Ms-Equ
, we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t
.
By definition of the subtype relation
\leq
, we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\leq t
.

Rule
Ws-Lf1
or
Ws-Lf2
:

In this case, we have as premises
\Gamma\vdash v^{\prime}\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}t
,

\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\vartriangleleft}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime}
.
By induction hypothesis, we obtain
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v^{\prime}\leq t
.
By definition of
\leq
, there exists
z
such that
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
.
The reduction
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\vartriangleleft}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime}
is
either
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime}
or
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime}
.
In the former case, by rule
Ms-Equ
, we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime}
.
Hence we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime}
.
Hence
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}v^{\prime}
.
We now have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}v^{\prime}
and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
, hence
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
.
By definition of
\leq
, we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\leq t
.

Rule
Ws-Rgh
:

In this case, we have as premises
\Gamma\vdash v\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}t^{\prime}
,

\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime}
.
By induction hypothesis, we obtain
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\leq t^{\prime}
.
By definition of
\leq
, there exists
z
such that
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
.
By definition of
\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}
, we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime}
and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t
.
Hence
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t^{\prime}
.
We now have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
, hence
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash z\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t^{\prime}
.
We have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t^{\prime}
and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
, hence
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
.
By definition of
\leq
, we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\leq t
.

∎

Proposition 14

(From well-equivalence to equivalence.)
.

Let
\Gamma
be a logical context and
u
and
v
be terms. If

\Gamma\vdash u\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}v
then
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\equiv v
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation tree of
\Gamma\vdash u\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.
We distinguish cases based on the last rule used in the derivation tree.

Rule
Ws-Rfl
:

In this case, we have
u=v=t
.
By Proposition
18
, we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t
.
By rule
Ms-Equ
, we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t
.
By definition of the subtype relation
\equiv
, we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\equiv t
.

Rule
Ws-Lf1
:

In this case, we have as premises
\Gamma\vdash v^{\prime}\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}t
,

\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime}
.
By induction hypothesis, we obtain
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v^{\prime}\equiv t
.
By definition of
\equiv
, there exists
z
such that
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
.
From
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime}
we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}v^{\prime}
.
We now have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}v^{\prime}
and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
, hence
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
.
By definition of
\equiv
, we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\equiv t
.

Rule
Ws-Rgh
:

In this case, we have as premises
\Gamma\vdash v\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}t^{\prime}
,

\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime}
.
By induction hypothesis, we obtain
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\equiv t^{\prime}
.
By definition of
\equiv
, there exists
z
such that
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
.
By definition of
\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}
, we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime}
and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t
.
Hence
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t^{\prime}
.
We now have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
, hence
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash z\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t^{\prime}
.
We have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t^{\prime}
and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
, hence
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
.
By definition of
\equiv
, we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\equiv t
.

∎

Lemma 15
.

Let
\Gamma
be a logical context and
u
and
v
be terms. If

\Gamma\vdash u\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}v
then
\Gamma\vdash v\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}u
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation tree of
\Gamma\vdash u\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.
We distinguish cases based on the last rule used in the derivation tree.

Rule
Ws-Rfl
:

In this case, we have
u=v
.
By rule
Ws-Rfl
, we have
\Gamma\vdash u\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}u
.

Rule
Ws-Lf1
:

In this case, we have as premises
\Gamma\vdash u^{\prime}\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}v
,

\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u^{\prime}
.
By induction hypothesis, we obtain
\Gamma\vdash v\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}u^{\prime}
.
By rule
Ws-Rgh
, we obtain
\Gamma\vdash v\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}u
.

Rule
Ws-Rgh
:

In this case, we have as premises
\Gamma\vdash u\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}v^{\prime}
,

\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime}
.
By induction hypothesis, we obtain
\Gamma\vdash v^{\prime}\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}u
.
By rule
Ws-Lf1
, we obtain
\Gamma\vdash v\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}u
.

∎

Lemma 16
.

Let
\Gamma
be a logical context and
u
and
v
be terms. If

\Gamma\vdash u\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}v
then
\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation tree of
\Gamma\vdash u\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.
We distinguish cases based on the last rule used in the derivation tree.

Rule
Ws-Rfl
:

In this case, we have
u=v
.
By rule
Ws-Rfl
, we have
\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}u
.

Rule
Ws-Lf1
:

In this case, we have as premises
\Gamma\vdash u^{\prime}\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}v
,

\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u^{\prime}
.
By induction hypothesis, we obtain
\Gamma\vdash u^{\prime}\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.
Hence by rule
Ws-Lf1
, we obtain
\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.

Rule
Ws-Rgh
:

In this case, we have as premises
\Gamma\vdash u\equiv_{\mathrm{wf}}v^{\prime}
,

\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime}
.
By induction hypothesis, we obtain
\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v^{\prime}
.
Hence by rule
Ws-Rgh
, we obtain
\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.

∎

Proposition 17

(From reduction semantics to equivalence reduction.)
.

Let
u
and
v
be terms.
If
u\mapsto v
then
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
for all extended context
\Gamma;s
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation tree of
u\mapsto v
. The base case is when the last
rule used is
Os-Bet
, and the result holds by rule
Me-Bet
and
reflexivity of
\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}
(Proposition
18
).

The induction cases is when the last rule used is
Os-Con
, and the result holds
by induction and rules
Me-Fun
,
Me-FOp
and
Me-App
.
∎

Proposition 18

(Reflexivity.)
.

Let
\Gamma;s
be an extended context, and
u
be a term,
we have
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u
, and
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u
.

Proof.

By straightforward induction on the derivation trees of

\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u
, noting that the
only applicable rules at the leaves are
Me-Top
and
Me-Var
,
and that at the rest of the nodes only rules

Me-App
,
Me-Fun
, and
Me-FOp
occur.
To now prove
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u
, we use rule
Ms-Equ
.
∎

Lemma 19

(Weakening of context)
.

Let
\Gamma;s
and
\Gamma^{\prime};s^{\prime}
be two extended contexts such that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}
is prevalid.
Let
u
and
v
be terms.
We have the following relations:

If
\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
.

If
\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.

If
\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.

If
\Gamma;s\vdash u\leq v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\leq v
.

If
\Gamma;s\vdash u\leq^{*}v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\leq^{*}v
.

If
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.

If
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}v
.

If
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.

If
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}v
.

Proof.

Note: this lemma is a group of different lemmas which are proved separately.
This proof dispatches the proof of each of these lemmas to their respective proof.

We do a case disjunction based on the case we are in:

Case
\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
or
\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v
or
\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v
:

Done in Lemma
20
.

Case
\Gamma;s\vdash u\leq v
:

We need to prove that if
\Gamma;s\vdash u\leq v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\leq v
.
By definition of the relation
\leq
, there exists a term
z
such that

\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
and
\Gamma;s\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
.
By using respectively Lemma
21
and Lemma
22

on respectively
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
and
\Gamma;s\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
,
we respectively obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
and
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}z
.
By definition of the relation
\leq
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\leq v
.

Case
\Gamma;s\vdash u\leq^{*}v
:

We need to prove that if
\Gamma;s\vdash u\leq^{*}v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\leq^{*}v
.
By definition of the relation
\leq^{*}
,
there exists
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}
such that

\Gamma;s\vdash u\leq u_{1}
,
\Gamma;s\vdash u_{1}\leq u_{2},\cdots,\Gamma;s\vdash u_{n}\leq v
.
By using the reasoning of the case above on each
\Gamma;s\vdash u_{i}\leq u_{i+1}
, we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\leq u_{1}
,
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u_{1}\leq u_{2},\cdots,\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u_{n}\leq v
.
By definition of the relation
\leq^{*}
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\leq^{*}v
.

Case
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
:

We need to prove that if
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.
We proceed by induction on the derivation tree of
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.
The proof can be found in Lemma
21
.

Case
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}v
:

We need to prove that if
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}v
.
By definition of the relation
\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}
, there exists
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}
such that

\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{1}
,
\Gamma;s\vdash u_{1}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{2},\cdots,\Gamma;s\vdash u_{n}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.
By using Lemma
21
multiple times, we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{1}
,
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u_{1}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{2},\cdots,\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u_{n}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.
By definition of the relation
\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}v
.

Case
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
:

We need to prove that if
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.
We proceed by induction on the derivation tree of
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.
The proof can be found in Lemma
22
.

Case
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}v
:

We need to prove that if
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}v
.
By definition of the relation
\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}
, there exists
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}
such that

\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{1}
,
\Gamma;s\vdash u_{1}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{2},\cdots,\Gamma;s\vdash u_{n}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.
By using Lemma
22
multiple times, we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{1}
,
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash u_{1}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u_{2},\cdots,\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash u_{n}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.
By definition of the relation
\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}v
.

∎

Lemma 20

(Weakening of context - Aux)
.

Let
\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
be two logical contexts such that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
is prevalid.
Let
u
and
v
be terms.
We have the following relations:

1.

If
\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
.

2.

If
\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.

3.

If
\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.

Proof.

We need to prove all of these relations together, because any derivation tree of any of these relations
will contain a derivation tree of the other relations.
Consider for instance
\Gamma\vdash(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,\mathsf{Top}.x)\,\mathsf{Top}~\mathrm{wf}
.
One of the premise of this derivation tree is
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{Top}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\mathsf{Top}
,
which will ultimately have as premise
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{Top}\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}\mathsf{Top}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{Top}~\mathrm{wf}
.
The proof therefore consider all of these relations together.
The proof works by induction on the derivation tree,
for the example above for instance,
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{Top}~\mathrm{wf}
is considered a base case, and therefore
we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathsf{Top}~\mathrm{wf}
, from which we will be able to conclude
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathsf{Top}\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}\mathsf{Top}
,
and so on.

Assume any of the cases of this lemma.
We do an induction on the size of the derivation tree of either

(\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
,
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{2}
or
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{3}

which we index by
i
for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
as a indexed family,
one of the three possible cases of this lemma.
The induction hypothesis can be called on any element of this family, as long as the size of its derivation tree
is smaller.
For instance, consider a derivation tree ending by the rule
Wf-App

(for instance
(\Gamma\vdash u\,v~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
), its premises are
derivations of the form
(\Gamma\vdash v\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}w)_{3}
, on which we can apply the induction hypothesis
because their size are smaller despite not being in the same index.

Suppose we are now in the case
1
, that is
(\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
, let’s prove
(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
.
We distinguish cases based on the last rule used in the derivation tree
(\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
.

Rule
Wf-PrS
or rule
Wf-PrE
with
u=x
:

In this case, we have
x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t\in\Gamma
,
and therefore
x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t\in\Gamma\cup\Gamma^{\prime}
.
By assumption, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}
prevalid, hence
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
prevalid.
Hence by rule
Wf-PrS
or rule
Wf-PrE
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash x~\mathrm{wf}
.

Rule
Wf-Top
:

In this case, we have as premises
\Gamma~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
By assumption, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}
prevalid, hence
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
prevalid.
We now have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathsf{Top}~\mathrm{wf}
by rule
Wf-Top
.

Rule
Wf-Fun
with
u=\lambda x\,{\leq}\,a.b
:

In this case, we have as premises
(\Gamma\vdash a~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
and
(\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,a\vdash b~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
.
Because
x
is a fresh variable, we can always alpha convert it to another name that does not exist already,
and as such the variable
x
is not used in
\Gamma^{\prime}
: no annotation of
x
exist in
\Gamma^{\prime}
,
and all the annotations of
\Gamma^{\prime}
does not reference
x
.
By induction hypothesis on the subtree
(\Gamma\vdash a~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
of our assumption
(\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
,
we obtain
(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
.
By induction hypothesis on the subtree
(\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,a\vdash b~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
of our assumption
(\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
,
we obtain
(\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,a,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash b~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
.
Because
x
does not exist in
\Gamma^{\prime}
, we have
(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},x\,{\leq}\,a\vdash b~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
.
By rule
Wf-Fun
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,a.b~\mathrm{wf}
.

Rule
Wf-App
with
u=a\,b
:

In this case, we have as premises
(\Gamma\vdash a\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,w.\mathsf{Top})_{3}
and
(\Gamma\vdash b\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}w)_{3}
.
By induction hypothesis on the subtree
(\Gamma\vdash a\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,w.\mathsf{Top})_{3}
of our assumption
(\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
,
we obtain
(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,w.\mathsf{Top})_{3}
.
By induction hypothesis on the subtree
(\Gamma\vdash b\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}w)_{3}
of our assumption
(\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
,
we obtain
(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash b\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}w)_{3}
.
By rule
Wf-App
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\,b~\mathrm{wf}
.

Suppose we are now in the case
2
, that is
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{2}
, let’s prove
(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{2}
.
We distinguish cases based on the last rule used in the derivation tree
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{2}
.

Rule
Ws-Rfl
:

In this case, we have
u=v=t
.
By using the induction hypothesis on our assumption
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{2}
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}t
.

Rule
Ws-Lf1
:

In this case, we have as premises
(\Gamma\vdash v^{\prime}\vartriangleleft_{\mathrm{wf}}t)_{2}
,

(\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime})_{i}
.
By induction hypothesis on the subtree
(\Gamma\vdash v^{\prime}\vartriangleleft_{\mathrm{wf}}t)_{2}
of our assumption
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{2}
,
we obtain
(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash v^{\prime}\vartriangleleft_{\mathrm{wf}}t)_{2}
.
By using Lemma
22

we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime}

from
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime}
.
By rule
Ws-Lf1
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash v\vartriangleleft_{\mathrm{wf}}t
.

Rule
Ws-Lf2
:

In this case, we have as premises
(\Gamma\vdash v^{\prime}\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}t)_{2}
,

(\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime})_{i}
,
(\Gamma\vdash v~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
and
(\Gamma\vdash v^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
.
By induction hypothesis on the subtree
(\Gamma\vdash v^{\prime}\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}t)_{2}
of our assumption
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{2}
,
we obtain
(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash v^{\prime}\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}t)_{2}
.
By using the induction hypothesis on the subtree
(\Gamma\vdash v~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
of our assumption
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{2}
,
we obtain
(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash v~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
.
By using the induction hypothesis on the subtree
(\Gamma\vdash v^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
of our assumption
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{2}
,
we obtain
(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash v^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf})_{1}
.
By using Lemma
21
,
we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime}

from
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime}
.
By rule
Ws-Lf2
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash v\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}t
.

Rule
Ws-Rgh
:

In this case, we have as premises
(\Gamma\vdash v\vartriangleleft_{\mathrm{wf}}t^{\prime})_{2}
,

(\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime})_{i}
.
By induction hypothesis on the subtree
(\Gamma\vdash v\vartriangleleft_{\mathrm{wf}}t^{\prime})_{2}
of our assumption
(\Gamma\vdash u\vartriangleleft_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{2}
,
we obtain
(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash v\vartriangleleft_{\mathrm{wf}}t^{\prime})_{2}
.
By using Lemma
22
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime}
.
By rule
Ws-Rgh
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash v\vartriangleleft_{\mathrm{wf}}t
.

Suppose we are now in the case
3
, that is
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{3}
, let’s prove
(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{3}
.
We distinguish cases based on the last rule used in the derivation tree
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{3}
.

Rule
Ws-Sub
:

In this case, we have as premises
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{2}
.
By using the induction hypothesis on the subtree
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{2}
of our assumption
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{3}
,
we obtain
(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{2}
.
By rule
Ws-Sub
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.

Rule
Ws-Trs
:

In this case, we have as premises
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}w)_{3}
and
(\Gamma\vdash w\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{3}
.
By induction hypothesis on the subtree
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}w)_{3}
of our assumption
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{3}
