,
we obtain
(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}w)_{3}
.
By induction hypothesis on the subtree
(\Gamma\vdash w\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{3}
of our assumption
(\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{3}
,
we obtain
(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash w\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v)_{3}
.
By rule
Ws-Trs
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.

∎

Lemma 21

(Weakening of context - Subtyping reduction)
.

Let
\Gamma;s
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
be two contexts such that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s
is prevalid.
Let
u
and
v
be terms.
We have the following relations:

If
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation tree of
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.
We distinguish based on the last rule used.

Rule
Ms-Pro
:

In this case, we have
u=x
and
v=t
with
x\,{\leq}\,t\in\Gamma
.
Because
x\,{\leq}\,t\in\Gamma
, we have
x\,{\leq}\,t\in\Gamma\cup\Gamma^{\prime}
.
By assumption, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}
prevalid, hence
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s
prevalid.
Hence by rule
Ms-Pro
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash x\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t
.

Rule
Ms-Top
:

In this case, we have
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}
and
the derivation has no premises other than the prevalidity of the context.
By assumption, we know that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s
is prevalid.
Hence, by rule
Ms-Top
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}
.

Rule
Ms-Equ
:

In this case, we have
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
with premise

\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.
By Lemma
22
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.
Hence, by rule
Ms-Equ
, we conclude that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.

Rule
Ms-App
:

In this case, we have
u=a\,b
and
v=a^{\prime}\,b
with premise

\Gamma;b::s\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};b::s\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
.
Hence, by rule
Ms-App
, we conclude that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash a\,b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}\,b
.

Rule
Ms-FOp
:

In this case,
u=\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.a
,
v=\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.a^{\prime}
and
s=\alpha::s_{0}

with premise
\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha;s_{0}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha;s_{0}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
.
From the fact that
x
is fresh
(hence can be renamed so that it never appears in
\Gamma^{\prime}
), we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha;s_{0}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
.
Therefore, by rule
Ms-FOp
, we deduce that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};\alpha::s_{0}\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.a^{\prime}
.

Rule
Ms-Fun
:

In this case,
u=\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.a
,
v=\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.a^{\prime}
and
s=\mathrm{nil}

with premise
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t;\mathrm{nil}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},x\,{\leq}\,t;\mathrm{nil}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
.
From the fact that
x
is fresh
(hence can be renamed so that it never appears in
\Gamma^{\prime}
), we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},x\,{\leq}\,t;\mathrm{nil}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
.
Therefore, by rule
Ms-Fun
, we deduce that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.a^{\prime}
.

∎

Lemma 22

(Weakening of context - Equivalence reduction)
.

Let
\Gamma;s
and
\Gamma^{\prime};s^{\prime}
be two extended contexts such that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}
is prevalid.
Let
u
and
v
be terms.
We have the following relations:

If
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation tree of
\Gamma;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.
We distinguish based on the last rule used.

Rule
Me-Var
:

In this case, we have
u=x
and
v=x
.
By assumption, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}
prevalid.
Hence by rule
Me-Var
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash x\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}x
.

Rule
Me-Pro
:

In this case, we have
u=x
and
v=\alpha^{\prime}
with
x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha^{\prime}\in\Gamma

and
\Gamma;s\vdash\alpha\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha^{\prime}
.
By induction, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash\alpha\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha^{\prime}
.
Because
x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha\in\Gamma
, we have
x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha\in\Gamma\cup\Gamma^{\prime}
.
By assumption, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}
prevalid.
Hence by rule
Me-Pro
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash x\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha^{\prime}
.

Rule
Me-App
:

In this case, we have
u=a\,b
and
v=a^{\prime}\,b^{\prime}
with premises

\Gamma;b::s\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis, we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};b::s@s^{\prime}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
.
Hence, by rule
Me-App
, we conclude that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash a\,b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}\,b^{\prime}
.

Rule
Me-Top
:

In this case, we have
\Gamma;s\vdash\mathsf{Top}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}
and
the derivation has no premises other than the prevalidity of the context.
By assumption, we know that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}
is prevalid.
Hence, by rule
Me-Top
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash\mathsf{Top}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}
.

Rule
Me-Fun
:

In this case,
u=\lambda x\,{\leq}\,a.b
,
v=\lambda x\,{\leq}\,a^{\prime}.b^{\prime}
and
s=\mathrm{nil}

with premises
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
and

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,a;\mathrm{nil}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
and

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,a,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
.
From the fact that
x
is fresh
(hence can be renamed so that it never appears in
\Gamma^{\prime}
), we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},x\,{\leq}\,a;\mathrm{nil}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
.
Therefore, by rule
Me-Fun
, we deduce that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,a.b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,a^{\prime}.b^{\prime}
.

Rule
Me-FOp
:

In this case,
u=\lambda x\,{\leq}\,a.b
,
v=\lambda x\,{\leq}\,a^{\prime}.b^{\prime}
and
s=\alpha::s_{0}

with premises
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
and

\Gamma;x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha;s_{0}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
and

\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha,\Gamma^{\prime};s_{0}@s^{\prime}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
.
From the fact that
x
is fresh
(hence can be renamed so that it never appears in
\Gamma^{\prime}
), we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha;s_{0}@s^{\prime}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
.
Therefore, by rule
Me-FOp
, we deduce that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};\alpha::s_{0}@s^{\prime}\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,a.b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,a^{\prime}.b^{\prime}
.

Rule
Me-Bet
:

In this case,
u=(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,a.b)\,c
and
v=b^{\prime}[x\backslash c^{\prime}]

with premises
\Gamma;s\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
and

\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash c\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}c^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash c\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}c^{\prime}
.
Therefore, by rule
Me-Bet
, we deduce that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash(\lambda x\,{\leq}\,a.b)\,c\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash c^{\prime}]
.

Rule
Me-TAp
:

In this case,
u=\mathsf{Top}\,b
and
v=\mathsf{Top}
with premise
\Gamma;s~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
By assumption, we know that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}
is prevalid.
Hence, by rule
Me-TAp
, we obtain
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime};s@s^{\prime}\vdash\mathsf{Top}\,b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}
.

∎

Lemma 23

(Narrowing of context in well-formedness derivation)
.

Let
\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
be two contexts.
Let
t
,
t^{\prime}
, and
u
be terms.
If
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime}
and
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf}
,
then
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation tree of
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
.
We distinguish cases on the last rule used in the derivation tree.

Rule
Wf-PrS
or rule
Wf-PrE
with
u=y
:

By assumption, we have
y\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t_{y}\in\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}
for some
t_{y}
,
and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
By Lemma
26
,
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
Either this subtype annotation is
x\,{\leq}\,t
, or it’s another one.
In the former case, we have
y=x
,
and
x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime}\in\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}
, hence
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}

by rule
Wf-PrS
.
In the latter case, we have
y\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t_{y}\in\Gamma\cup\Gamma^{\prime}
,
hence
y\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t_{y}\in\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}

hence
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}
by rule
Wf-PrS
or
Wf-PrE
.

Rule
Wf-Top
:

By assumption,
u=\mathsf{Top}
and

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
By Lemma
26
,
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
Hence,
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathsf{Top}~\mathrm{wf}
by rule
Wf-Top
.

Rule
Wf-Fun
with
u=\lambda y\,{\leq}\,v.w
:

We have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\lambda y\,{\leq}\,v.w~\mathrm{wf}
with premise

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime},y\,{\leq}\,v\vdash w~\mathrm{wf}
.
By the induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime},y\,{\leq}\,v\vdash w~\mathrm{wf}
.
Hence,
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\lambda y\,{\leq}\,v.w~\mathrm{wf}
by
Wf-Fun
.

Rule
Wf-App
with
u=a\,b
:

By assumption, there exist some term
z
such that

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.\mathsf{Top}
and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash b\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}z
.
We prove the case
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.\mathsf{Top}
, the other one is done by the exact same reasoning.
We wish to prove
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.\mathsf{Top}

We first decompose
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.\mathsf{Top}

into
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}a_{0}\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}\cdots\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}a_{n}\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.\mathsf{Top}
,
to then prove we can transform each of these step of this derivation

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash b\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}c
into

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash b\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}c
.
To do so, we do the same reasoning as in Lemma
7
by using instead Lemmas

24
and
25
.
Hence by using the rule
Ws-Trs
, we later obtain
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.\mathsf{Top}
.
By using the same reasoning on
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash b\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}z
,
we obtain
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash b\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}z
.
Hence
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\,b~\mathrm{wf}
by rule
Wf-App
.

∎

Lemma 24

(Narrowing of context in subtyping reductions)
.

Let
\Gamma;s
be an extended context, let
\Gamma^{\prime}
be an additional context.
Let
u
,
v
,
t
and
t^{\prime}
be terms.
If
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime}
.
Assume that both
u
,
v
are well-formed in context
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}
.
Assume also
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf}
.
Then there exists a term
v^{\prime}
such that we have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime}
,
and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime}
,
and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash v^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf}
.

Proof.

If the derivation
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v

does not make a promotion of
x
to
t
, then we have

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v

and
v^{\prime}=v
satisfy the lemma.

Else, if the derivation
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v

make a promotion of
x
to
t
, then we have

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\mathsf{Co}[x]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Co}[t^{\prime}]
with

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\mathsf{Co}[t^{\prime}]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Co}[t]

by weakening to adapt the context and obtain the last derivation from
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime}
.
We now need to show that
v^{\prime}
is well-formed in context
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}

by induction on the structure of
v^{\prime}
.

Case
v^{\prime}=x
:

A variable is well-formed by rule
Wf-PrS
or
Wf-PrE
.

Case
v^{\prime}=\mathsf{Top}
:

We have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathsf{Top}~\mathrm{wf}
by rule
Wf-Top
.

Case
v^{\prime}=\lambda y\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime\prime}.w
:

We have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\lambda y\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime\prime}.w~\mathrm{wf}

by rule
Wf-Fun
with premise
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime},y\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime\prime}\vdash w~\mathrm{wf}
.
By the induction hypothesis, we have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime},y\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime\prime}\vdash w~\mathrm{wf}
.

Case
v^{\prime}=a\,b
:

Because
v^{\prime}
and
u
are the same term, except for a promotion of
x
to
t
in head position,
we know in our induction that
u=a^{\prime}\,b^{\prime}
with
b^{\prime}=b
.
Because
u
is well-formed, we have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w.\mathsf{Top}

and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash b^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}w
.
We now prove
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w.\mathsf{Top}
,
which will allow us to conclude that
v^{\prime}
is well-formed by rule
Wf-App
.
We have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w.\mathsf{Top}
.
By using the induction hypothesis on
a
, we know that it’s well-formed in context
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}
.
Because
a^{\prime}
is
a
but with a promotion of
x
to
t
in head position, we have

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}a^{\prime}
from rule
Ws-Lf2
.
Hence we can conclude that
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w.\mathsf{Top}
.

∎

Lemma 25

(Narrowing of context in equivalence reductions)
.

Let
\Gamma;s
be an extended context, let
\Gamma^{\prime}
be an additional context.
Let
u
,
v
,
t
and
t^{\prime}
be terms.
If
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime}
.
Then we have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.

Proof.

We do an induction on the derivation tree of
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.
We distinguish cases on the last rule used.

Rule
Me-Var
:

We have
u=v=y
some variable.
The derivation
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
hence holds by rule
Me-Var
.

Rule
Me-Pro
:

We have
u=y
and
v=\alpha^{\prime}
with
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha\in\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\alpha\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha^{\prime}
.
By prevalidity of the context, we know that
y\neq x
, and as a result we have
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha\in\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
,
hence
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha\in\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}
.
By rule
Me-Pro
, we therefore have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash y\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha^{\prime}
.

Rule
Me-Top
:

By assumption, we have

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathsf{Top}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}
with premise

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
By Lemma
26
,
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};s~\mathrm{prevalid}
.

By rule
Ms-Top
, we have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash\mathsf{Top}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}
.

Rule
Me-App
:

We have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash a\,b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}\,b^{\prime}
with premises
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};b::s\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};b::s\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
.
Hence
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash a\,b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}\,b^{\prime}
by
Ms-App
.

Rule
Me-Fun
:

We have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w.u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w^{\prime}.u^{\prime}

with
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime},y\,{\leq}\,w;\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u^{\prime}
and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash w\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}w^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime},y\,{\leq}\,w;\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u^{\prime}

and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash w\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}w^{\prime}
.
Thus,
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w.u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w^{\prime}.u^{\prime}
by
Me-Fun
.

Rule
Me-FOp
:

We have

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\alpha::s\vdash\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w.u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w^{\prime}.u^{\prime}

with premise
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime},y\equiv\alpha;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u^{\prime}
and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash w\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}w^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime},y\equiv\alpha;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u^{\prime}
and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash w\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}w^{\prime}
.
Hence
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w.u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w^{\prime}.u^{\prime}
by
Me-FOp
.

Rule
Me-Bet
:

We have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a.b)\,c\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}[y\backslash c^{\prime}]
with premises

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash c\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}c^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash c\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}c^{\prime}
.
Hence
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a.b)\,c\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}[y\backslash c^{\prime}]
by
Me-Bet
.

Rule
Me-TAp
:

We have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash\mathsf{Top}\,b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}
with premise
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
By Lemma
26
,
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};s~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
Hence
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathsf{Top}\,b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}
by
Me-TAp
.

∎

Lemma 26

(Narrowing prevalidity)
.

Let
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}s
be an extended prevalid context,
and
t^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma;s\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime}
.
Then
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};s
is prevalid.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation tree of
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}s~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
We distinguish cases on the last rule used.

Rule
Pv-Emp
:

In this case, we have
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}=\varepsilon
.
Hence
\Gamma=\varepsilon
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\varepsilon
.
By assumption, we have
\Gamma;s\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime}
.
We need to prove that
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};s
is prevalid,
which is equivalent to proving that
x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime};s
is prevalid.
Because
\Gamma=\varepsilon
, we have
\mathrm{fv}(t^{\prime})=\emptyset
.
Hence
x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime};s
is prevalid.

Rule
Pv-Ctx
:

In this case, we have
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{0},y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}

with
\Gamma_{0}~\mathrm{prevalid}
and
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{0})
and
\mathrm{fv}(t_{y})\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{0})
.
Either
y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}
is
x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t
, or it is in
\Gamma_{0}
or
\Gamma^{\prime}
.

If
y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}
is
x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t
, then
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\varepsilon
.
By assumption, we have
\Gamma;s\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime}
.
We need to prove that
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};s
is prevalid,
which is equivalent to proving that
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime};s
is prevalid.
Because
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma
, we have
\Gamma~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
Because
x\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma)
, we have
x\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma)
.
Because
\mathrm{fv}(t)\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma)
and
\Gamma;s\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime}
,
we have
\mathrm{fv}(t^{\prime})\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma)
.
Hence
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime};s
is prevalid.

If
y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}
is in
\Gamma_{0}
, then
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma_{1},y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}
for some
\Gamma_{1}
.
We have
\Gamma_{1}~\mathrm{prevalid}
and
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
\mathrm{fv}(t_{y})\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
.
We also have
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\varepsilon
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma_{1},x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};s
is prevalid.
Hence
\Gamma_{1},x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime},y\,{\leq}\,t_{y};s
is prevalid.

If
y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}
is in
\Gamma^{\prime}
, then
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1},y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}
for some
\Gamma_{1}
.
We have
\Gamma_{1}~\mathrm{prevalid}
and
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
\mathrm{fv}(t_{y})\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
.
We also have
\Gamma=\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1},y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma_{1};s
is prevalid.
Hence
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma_{1},y\,{\leq}\,t_{y};s
is prevalid.

Rule
Pv-EqA
:

In this case, we have
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{0},y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha

with
\Gamma_{0}~\mathrm{prevalid}
and
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{0})
and
\mathrm{fv}(\alpha)\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{0})
.
Either
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha
is
x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t
, or it is in
\Gamma_{0}
or
\Gamma^{\prime}
.

If
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha
is
x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t
, then
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\varepsilon
.
By assumption, we have
\Gamma;s\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime}
.
We need to prove that
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};s
is prevalid,
which is equivalent to proving that
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime};s
is prevalid.
Because
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma
, we have
\Gamma~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
Because
x\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma)
, we have
x\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma)
.
Because
\mathrm{fv}(t)\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma)
and
\Gamma;s\vdash t\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t^{\prime}
,
we have
\mathrm{fv}(t^{\prime})\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma)
.
Hence
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime};s
is prevalid.

If
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha
is in
\Gamma_{0}
, then
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma_{1},y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha
for some
\Gamma_{1}
.
We have
\Gamma_{1}~\mathrm{prevalid}
and
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
\mathrm{fv}(\alpha)\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
.
We also have
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\varepsilon
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma_{1},x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};s
is prevalid.
Hence
\Gamma_{1},x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime},y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha;s
is prevalid.

If
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha
is in
\Gamma^{\prime}
, then
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1},y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha
for some
\Gamma_{1}
.
We have
\Gamma_{1}~\mathrm{prevalid}
and
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
\mathrm{fv}(\alpha)\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
.
We also have
\Gamma=\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1},y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma_{1};s
is prevalid.
Hence
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma_{1},y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha;s
is prevalid.

Rule
Pv-Nil
:

In this case, we have
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s=\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}

with
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
Hence
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}
is prevalid.

Rule
Pv-Sta
:

In this case, we have

\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s=\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\alpha::s_{0}

with
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s_{0}~\mathrm{prevalid}
and
\mathrm{fv}(\alpha)\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime})
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};s_{0}~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
Because
\mathrm{fv}(\alpha)\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime})
,
we have
\mathrm{fv}(\alpha)\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime})
.
Hence
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime};\alpha::s_{0}
is prevalid.

∎

Proposition 27

(Reduction preserves subtyping derivation)
.

Let
\Gamma;s
be an extended context.
Let
u
,
u^{\prime}
and
v
be terms such that

\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v
and

u\mapsto u^{\prime}
, and

\Gamma\vdash u^{\prime}~\mathrm{wf}
.

Then
\Gamma\vdash u^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.

Proof.

By
17
, we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u^{\prime}
.
By rule
Ws-Rfl
and
Ws-Lf1
, we have
\Gamma\vdash u^{\prime}\leq_{\mathrm{wf}}u
.
From Proposition
12
, we have
\Gamma\vdash u~\mathrm{wf}

from
\Gamma\vdash u\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.
Hence rule
Ws-Sub
, we have
\Gamma\vdash u^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}u
.
By
Ws-Trs
, we then have
\Gamma\vdash u^{\prime}\leq^{*}_{\mathrm{wf}}v
.
∎

Lemma 28

(Substitution preserves prevalidity)
.

Let
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s
be an extended context.

If
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s
is prevalid, then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t];s[x\backslash t]
is prevalid.

If
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}
is prevalid, then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t]
is prevalid.

Proof.

We prove the first result, the second is similar.
We now do an induction on the derivation tree
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s
prevalid.
We distinguish cases on the last rule used in the derivation tree of
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s
prevalid.

Rule
Pv-Emp
:

In this case, we have
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}=\varepsilon
.
Hence
\Gamma=\varepsilon
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\varepsilon
.
The result to prove in this case is
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t]=\varepsilon
is prevalid,
which holds by rule
Pv-Emp
.

Rule
Pv-Ctx
:

In this case, we have
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{0},y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}

with
\Gamma_{0}~\mathrm{prevalid}
and
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{0})
and
\mathrm{fv}(t_{y})\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{0})
.
Either
y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}
is
x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t
, or it is in
\Gamma_{0}
or
\Gamma^{\prime}
.

If
y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}
is
x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t
, then
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\varepsilon
.
We need to prove that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t]=\Gamma
is prevalid,
which holds by assumption.

If
y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}
is in
\Gamma_{0}
, then
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma_{1},y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}
for some
\Gamma_{1}
.
We have
\Gamma_{1}~\mathrm{prevalid}
and
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
\mathrm{fv}(t_{y})\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
.
We also have
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\varepsilon
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t]=\Gamma_{1}
is prevalid.
Because
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
, we have
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t])
.
Because
\mathrm{fv}(t_{y})\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
, we have
\mathrm{fv}(t_{y}[x\backslash t])\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t])
.
Hence
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t],y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}[x\backslash t]
is prevalid.

If
y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}
is in
\Gamma^{\prime}
, then
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1},y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}
for some
\Gamma_{1}
.
We have
\Gamma_{1}~\mathrm{prevalid}
and
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
\mathrm{fv}(t_{y})\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
.
We also have
\Gamma=\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1},y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,\Gamma_{1}[x\backslash t]
is prevalid.
Because
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
, we have
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma,\Gamma_{1}[x\backslash t])
.
Because
\mathrm{fv}(t_{y})\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
, we have
\mathrm{fv}(t_{y}[x\backslash t])\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma,\Gamma_{1}[x\backslash t])
.
Hence
\Gamma,\Gamma_{1}[x\backslash t],y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}[x\backslash t]
is prevalid.

Rule
Pv-EqA
:

In this case, we have
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{0},y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha

with
\Gamma_{0}~\mathrm{prevalid}
and
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{0})
and
\mathrm{fv}(\alpha)\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{0})
.
Either
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha
is
x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t
, or it is in
\Gamma_{0}
or
\Gamma^{\prime}
.

If
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha
is
x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t
, then
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\varepsilon
.
We need to prove that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t]=\Gamma
is prevalid,
which holds by assumption.

If
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha
is in
\Gamma_{0}
, then
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma_{1},y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha
for some
\Gamma_{1}
.
We have
\Gamma_{1}~\mathrm{prevalid}
and
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
\mathrm{fv}(\alpha)\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
.
We also have
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\varepsilon
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t]=\Gamma_{1}
is prevalid.
Because
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
, we have
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t])
.
Because
\mathrm{fv}(\alpha)\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
, we have
\mathrm{fv}(\alpha[x\backslash t])\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t])
.
Hence
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t],y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha[x\backslash t]
is prevalid.

If
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha
is in
\Gamma^{\prime}
, then
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1},y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha
for some
\Gamma_{1}
.
We have
\Gamma_{1}~\mathrm{prevalid}
and
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
\mathrm{fv}(\alpha)\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
.
We also have
\Gamma=\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1},y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,\Gamma_{1}[x\backslash t]
is prevalid.
Because
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
, we have
y\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma,\Gamma_{1}[x\backslash t])
.
Because
\mathrm{fv}(\alpha)\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
, we have
\mathrm{fv}(\alpha[x\backslash t])\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma,\Gamma_{1}[x\backslash t])
.
Hence
\Gamma,\Gamma_{1}[x\backslash t],y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha[x\backslash t]
is prevalid.

Rule
Pv-Nil
:

In this case, we have
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s=\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}

with
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t]~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
Hence
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t];\mathrm{nil}
is prevalid.

Rule
Pv-Sta
:

In this case, we have

\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s=\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\alpha::s_{0}

with
\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s_{0}~\mathrm{prevalid}
and
\mathrm{fv}(\alpha)\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime})
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t];s_{0}[x\backslash t]~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
Because
\mathrm{fv}(\alpha)\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma,x\,{\vartriangleleft}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime})
,
we have
\mathrm{fv}(\alpha[x\backslash t])\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t])
.
Hence
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash t];\alpha[x\backslash t]::s_{0}[x\backslash t]
is prevalid.

∎

Lemma 29

(Promotion under substitution outside of covariant contexts)
.

Let
\Gamma;s
be an extended context, and
\Gamma^{\prime}
be a logical context,
such that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}
is prevalid.
Let
t
and
\alpha
be terms.

Let
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\mathsf{Co}[x]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Co}[t]
be a derivation
for some covariant context
\mathsf{Co}
.

Then
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash\mathsf{Co}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[x]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Co}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[t]
.

Proof.

By induction on
u
. We distinguish cases based on the structure of
u
.

u=\mathsf{Top}
:

Impossible by assumption.

u=x
:

Nothing to do.

u=\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.u^{\prime}
:

By assumption, we have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\mathsf{Co}[\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.u^{\prime}]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Co}[\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.v]
.

Let
P^{\prime}
be such that
\mathsf{Co}[\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.u^{\prime}]=\mathsf{Co}^{\prime}[u^{\prime}]
.

Our derivation is now
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\mathsf{Co}^{\prime}[u^{\prime}]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Co}^{\prime}[v]
.

By induction, we have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash\mathsf{Co}^{\prime}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[u^{\prime}]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Co}^{\prime}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[v]
.

By definition of
\mathsf{Co}^{\prime}
, this is

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash\mathsf{Co}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.u^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Co}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.v[x\backslash\alpha]]
.

To conclude we need to prove that

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash\mathsf{Co}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z[x\backslash\alpha].u^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Co}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z[x\backslash\alpha].v[x\backslash\alpha]]

holds.

Because this derivation make a promotion of
x
to
t
, it doesn’t do a promotion of
y
to its type annotation,
as a result this derivation holds.

By alpha conversion, the term
\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z[x\backslash\alpha].u^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]

is equal to
(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z.u^{\prime})[x\backslash\alpha]
, and similarly for
\lambda y\,{\leq}\,z[x\backslash\alpha].v[x\backslash\alpha]
.

Hence the result.

u=u^{\prime}\,u^{\prime\prime}
:

By assumption, we have

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\mathsf{Co}[u^{\prime}\,u^{\prime\prime}]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Co}[v^{\prime}\,v^{\prime\prime}]
.

Let
P^{\prime}
be such that
\mathsf{Co}[u^{\prime}\,u^{\prime\prime}]=\mathsf{Co}^{\prime}[u^{\prime}]
.

Our derivation is now
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash\mathsf{Co}^{\prime}[](u^{\prime})\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Co}^{\prime}[](v^{\prime})
.

By induction, we have
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash(\mathsf{Co}^{\prime}[])[x\backslash\alpha](u^{\prime})\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}(\mathsf{Co}^{\prime}[])[x\backslash\alpha](v^{\prime})
.

By definition of
P^{\prime}
, this is
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash\mathsf{Co}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[u^{\prime}\,u^{\prime\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Co}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[v^{\prime}\,v^{\prime\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]]
.

To conclude we need to prove that

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash\mathsf{Co}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[u^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\,u^{\prime\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Co}_{[x\backslash\alpha]}[v^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\,v^{\prime\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]]

holds.

Because this derivation make a promotion of
x
to
t
, it doesn’t use the annotation of
u^{\prime\prime}
in the stack,
as a result this derivation holds.

By definition of the substitution, the term
u^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\,u^{\prime\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]

is equal to
(u^{\prime}\,u^{\prime\prime})[x\backslash\alpha]
, and similarly for
v^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\,v^{\prime\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
,
hence the result.

∎

Lemma 30

(Promotion under substitution inside of covariant contexts)
.

Let
\Gamma;s
be an extended context.
Let
\Gamma^{\prime}
be a logical context,
such that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]
is prevalid.
Let
u
,
v
,
t
and
\alpha
be terms.
Let
x
be a variable.
If
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
,
such that this derivation is NOT of the form
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash\mathsf{Co}[x]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Co}[t]

for some covariant context
P
.
Then we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash u[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v[x\backslash\alpha]
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation tree of
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.
We distinguish cases on the last rule used.

Rule
Ms-Pro
:

In this case, we have
u=y
and
v=t_{y}
with
y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}\in\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}

and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}
prevalid.
We know that
y\not=x
from the assumption that our derivation does not make a promotion of
x
to
t
.
If
y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}\in\Gamma
, by prevalidity of
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}
we know
there is no instance of
x
in
t_{y}
, and as such we have
t_{y}[x\backslash\alpha]=t_{y}
.
Now we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash y\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t_{y}

by rule
Ms-Pro
, which is the desired result.
If
y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}\in\Gamma^{\prime}
, we have by definition of the substitution on context,

y\,{\leq}\,t_{y}[x\backslash\alpha]\in\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
,
and as a result we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash y\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}t_{y}[x\backslash\alpha]

by rule
Ms-Pro
.

Rule
Ms-Top
:

In this case, we have

u=y
and
v=\mathsf{Top}
.
We need to show
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash u[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Since
\mathsf{Top}[x\backslash\alpha]=\mathsf{Top}
,
we need to show
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash u[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}
,
which holds by rule
Ms-Top
.

Rule
Ms-Equ
:

In this case, we have

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.
By Lemma
31
,
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash u[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Hence,
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash u[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v[x\backslash\alpha]
by
Ms-Equ
.

Rule
Ms-App
:

In this case, we have
u=a\,b
and
v=a^{\prime}\,b
with premise
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};b::s\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];b[x\backslash\alpha]::s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Hence
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\,b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\,b[x\backslash\alpha]
by
Ms-App
,
which is by definition of the substitution,
equivalent to
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash(a\,b)[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}(a^{\prime}\,b)[x\backslash\alpha]
.

Rule
Ms-Fun
:

In this case,
u=\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w.a
,
v=\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w.a^{\prime}
with

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime},y\,{\leq}\,w;\mathrm{nil}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha],y\,{\leq}\,w[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Thus,
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w[x\backslash\alpha].a[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w[x\backslash\alpha].a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
by
Ms-Fun
,
which is by definition of the substitution, and because by alpha conversion we have
y\not=x
,
equivalent to
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w.a)[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w.a^{\prime})[x\backslash\alpha]
.

Rule
Ms-FOp
:

In this case,
u=\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w.a
,
v=\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w.a^{\prime}
with
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime},y\equiv\beta;s\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha],y\equiv\beta[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Thus,
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w[x\backslash\alpha].a[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w[x\backslash\alpha].a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
by
Ms-FOp
,
which is by definition of the substitution, and because by alpha conversion we have
y\not=x
,
equivalent to
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w.a)[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,w.a^{\prime})[x\backslash\alpha]
.

∎

Lemma 31

(Reduction under substitution)
.

Let
\Gamma;s
be an extended context.
Let
\Gamma^{\prime}
be a logical context,
such that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]
is prevalid.
Let
u
,
v
,
t
and
\alpha
be terms.
Let
x
be a variable.
If
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
,
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash u[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v[x\backslash\alpha]
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation tree of
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.
We distinguish cases on the last rule used.

Rule
Me-Var
:

In this case, we have
u=y
and
v=y
with
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}
prevalid.
If
y=x
, then
u[x\backslash\alpha]=\alpha
and
v[x\backslash\alpha]=\alpha
.
We need to show
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash\alpha\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha
,
which holds by reflexivity (Proposition
18
).
If
y\neq x
, then
u[x\backslash\alpha]=y
and
v[x\backslash\alpha]=y
.
We need to show
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash y\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}y
,
which holds by rule
Me-Var
.

Rule
Me-Pro
:

In this case, we have
u=y
and
v=\alpha^{\prime\prime}

with
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha^{\prime}\in\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}
,
and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\alpha^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha^{\prime\prime}
,
and
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}
prevalid.
Because
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}
is prevalid, there is a single annotation of
x
,
and because it’s
x\,{\leq}\,t
it cannot be
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha^{\prime}
and as such we have
y\not=x
.
If
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha^{\prime}\in\Gamma
, by prevalidity of
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime}
we know
there is no instance of
x
in
\alpha^{\prime\prime}
, and as such we have
\alpha^{\prime\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]=\alpha^{\prime}
and

y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha^{\prime}\in\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Now we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash y\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha^{\prime\prime}

by rule
Me-Pro
, which is the desired result.
If
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{\prime}
, we have by definition of the substitution on context,

y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\in\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
,
hence
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\in\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
By induction, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash\alpha^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha^{\prime\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
,
hence by rule
Me-Pro
, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash y\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha^{\prime\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.

Rule
Me-App
:

In this case, we have
u=a\,b
and

v=a^{\prime}\,b^{\prime}
with premises

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};b::s\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
and

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis, we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];b[x\backslash\alpha]::s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Hence, by rule
Me-App
, we conclude that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\,b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]\,b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.

Rule
Me-Top
:

In this case, we have
u=\mathsf{Top}
and

v=\mathsf{Top}
.
We need to show
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash\mathsf{Top}[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Since
\mathsf{Top}[x\backslash\alpha]=\mathsf{Top}
,
we need to show
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash\mathsf{Top}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}
,
which holds by rule
Me-Top
.

Rule
Me-Fun
:

In this case,
u=\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a.b
,

v=\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a^{\prime}.b^{\prime}
and
s=\mathrm{nil}

with premises
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
and

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime},y\,{\leq}\,a;\mathrm{nil}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha],y\,{\leq}\,a[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Therefore, by rule
Me-Fun
, we deduce that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a[x\backslash\alpha].b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha].b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
,
which is by definition of the substitution, and because by alpha conversion we have
y\not=x
,

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a.b)[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a^{\prime}.b^{\prime})[x\backslash\alpha]
.

Rule
Me-FOp
:

In this case,
u=\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a.b
,

v=\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a^{\prime}.b^{\prime}
and
s=\beta::s_{0}

with premises
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
and

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};y\,{\equiv}\,\beta;s_{0}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
.

By the induction hypothesis we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha],y\,{\equiv}\,\beta[x\backslash\alpha];s_{0}[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Therefore, by rule
Me-FOp
, we deduce that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];(\beta::s_{0})[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a[x\backslash\alpha].b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha].b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
,
which is by definition of the substitution, and because by alpha conversion we have
y\not=x
,

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a.b)[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a^{\prime}.b^{\prime})[x\backslash\alpha]
.

Rule
Me-Bet
:

In this case,
u=(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a.b)\,c
and

v=b^{\prime}[y\backslash c^{\prime}]

with premises

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
and

\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash c\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}c^{\prime}
.

By the induction hypothesis we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];\mathrm{nil}\vdash c[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}c^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Therefore, by rule
Me-Bet
, we deduce that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a[x\backslash\alpha].b[x\backslash\alpha])\,c[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b[x\backslash\alpha][y\backslash c^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha]]
.

This last term, according to Barendregt’s substitution lemma
[
5
]
, is
b^{\prime}[y\backslash c^{\prime}][x\backslash\alpha]
,
Hence
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a.b)[x\backslash\alpha]\,c[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}[y\backslash c^{\prime}][x\backslash\alpha]
.

Rule
Me-TAp
:

In this case,
u=\mathsf{Top}\,b
and
v=\mathsf{Top}
with premise
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t,\Gamma^{\prime};s~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
We need to show
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash\mathsf{Top}\,b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}[x\backslash\alpha]
.
Since
\mathsf{Top}[x\backslash\alpha]=\mathsf{Top}
,
we need to show
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash\alpha];s[x\backslash\alpha]\vdash\mathsf{Top}\,b[x\backslash\alpha]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}
,
which holds by rule
Me-TAp
.

∎

Lemma 32

(Reduction under substitution - auxiliary for the commutation theorem)
.

Let
\Gamma;s
be an extended context.
Let
u
,
u^{\prime}
,
v
and
v^{\prime}
be terms.
Let
x
be a variable.
If
\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,v,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u^{\prime}
and
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v^{\prime}
,
then
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];s[x\backslash v]\vdash u[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}u^{\prime}[x\backslash v^{\prime}]
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation tree of
\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,v,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
.
We distinguish cases on the last rule used.

Rule
Me-Var
:

In this case, we have
u=y
and
v=y
with
\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,v,\Gamma^{\prime}
prevalid.
If
y=x
, then
u[x\backslash v]=v
and
v[x\backslash v]=v
.
We need to show
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];s[x\backslash v]\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}v
,
which holds by reflexivity (Proposition
18
).
If
y\neq x
, then
u[x\backslash v]=y
and
v[x\backslash v]=y
.
We need to show
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];s[x\backslash v]\vdash y\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}y
,
which holds by rule
Me-Var
.

Rule
Me-Pro
with
u=y\neq x
:

In this case, we have
u=y
and
v=\alpha^{\prime\prime}

with
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha^{\prime}\in\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,v,\Gamma^{\prime}
,
and
\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,v,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\alpha^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha^{\prime\prime}
,
and
\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,v,\Gamma^{\prime}
prevalid.
If
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha^{\prime}\in\Gamma
, by prevalidity of
\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,v,\Gamma^{\prime}
we know
there is no instance of
x
in
\alpha^{\prime\prime}
, and as such we have
\alpha^{\prime\prime}[x\backslash v]=\alpha^{\prime}
and

y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha^{\prime}\in\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v]
.
Now we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];s[x\backslash v]\vdash y\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha^{\prime\prime}

by rule
Me-Pro
, which is the desired result.
If
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{\prime}
, we have by definition of the substitution on context,

y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha^{\prime}[x\backslash v]\in\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v]
,
hence
y\,{\equiv}\,\alpha^{\prime}[x\backslash v]\in\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v]
.
By induction, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];s[x\backslash v]\vdash\alpha^{\prime}[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha^{\prime\prime}[x\backslash v]
,
hence by rule
Me-Pro
, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];s[x\backslash v]\vdash y\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha^{\prime\prime}[x\backslash v]
.

Rule
Me-Pro
with
u=x
:

In this case, we have
u=x
and
v=\alpha
with premise
\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,v,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha
.
By prevalidity, we know that there is no instance of
x
in both
v
and
\alpha
. As a result we have both
v[x\backslash v]=v
and
\alpha[x\backslash v]=\alpha
.
Therefore induction we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];s[x\backslash v]\vdash v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\alpha
.
Because
u[x\backslash v]=v
, this is the desired result.

Rule
Me-App
:

In this case, we have
u=a\,b
and

v=a^{\prime}\,b^{\prime}
with premises

\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,v,\Gamma^{\prime};b::s\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
and

\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,v,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis, we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];b[x\backslash v]::s[x\backslash v]\vdash a[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash v]
and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash v]
.
Hence, by rule
Me-App
, we conclude that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];s[x\backslash v]\vdash a[x\backslash v]\,b[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash v]\,b^{\prime}[x\backslash v]
.

Rule
Me-Top
:

In this case, we have
u=\mathsf{Top}
and

v=\mathsf{Top}
.
We need to show
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];s[x\backslash v]\vdash\mathsf{Top}[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}[x\backslash v]
.
Since
\mathsf{Top}[x\backslash v]=\mathsf{Top}
,
we need to show
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];s[x\backslash v]\vdash\mathsf{Top}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}
,
which holds by rule
Me-Top
.

Rule
Me-Fun
:

In this case,
u=\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a.b
,

v=\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a^{\prime}.b^{\prime}
and
s=\mathrm{nil}

with premises
\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,v,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
and

\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,v,\Gamma^{\prime},y\,{\leq}\,a;\mathrm{nil}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash v]
and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v],y\,{\leq}\,a[x\backslash v];\mathrm{nil}\vdash b[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash v]
.
Therefore, by rule
Me-Fun
, we deduce that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];\mathrm{nil}\vdash\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a[x\backslash v].b[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a^{\prime}[x\backslash v].b^{\prime}[x\backslash v]
,
which is by definition of the substitution, and because by alpha conversion we have
y\not=x
,

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];\mathrm{nil}\vdash(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a.b)[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a^{\prime}.b^{\prime})[x\backslash v]
.

Rule
Me-FOp
:

In this case,
u=\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a.b
,

v=\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a^{\prime}.b^{\prime}
and
s=\beta::s_{0}

with premises

\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,v,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}
and

\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,v,\Gamma^{\prime};y\,{\equiv}\,\beta;s_{0}\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
.

By the induction hypothesis we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];\mathrm{nil}\vdash a[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}a^{\prime}[x\backslash v]
and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v],y\,{\equiv}\,\beta[x\backslash v];s_{0}[x\backslash v]\vdash b[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash v]
.
Therefore, by rule
Me-FOp
, we deduce that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];(\beta::s_{0})[x\backslash v]\vdash\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a[x\backslash v].b[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a^{\prime}[x\backslash v].b^{\prime}[x\backslash v]
,
which is by definition of the substitution, and because by alpha conversion we have
y\not=x
,

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];s[x\backslash v]\vdash(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a.b)[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a^{\prime}.b^{\prime})[x\backslash v]
.

Rule
Me-Bet
:

In this case,
u=(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a.b)\,c

and
v=b^{\prime}[y\backslash c^{\prime}]

with premises

\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,v,\Gamma^{\prime};s\vdash b\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}
and

\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,v,\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}\vdash c\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}c^{\prime}
.

By the induction hypothesis we obtain

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];s[x\backslash v]\vdash b[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}[x\backslash v]
and

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];\mathrm{nil}\vdash c[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}c^{\prime}[x\backslash v]
.
Therefore, by rule
Me-Bet
, we deduce that

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];s[x\backslash v]\vdash(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a[x\backslash v].b[x\backslash v])\,c[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b[x\backslash v][y\backslash c^{\prime}[x\backslash v]]
.

This last term, according to Barendregt’s substitution lemma
[
5
]
, is
b^{\prime}[y\backslash c^{\prime}][x\backslash v]
,

Hence
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];s[x\backslash v]\vdash(\lambda y\,{\leq}\,a.b)[x\backslash v]\,c[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b^{\prime}[y\backslash c^{\prime}][x\backslash v]
.

Rule
Me-TAp
:

In this case,
u=\mathsf{Top}\,b
and
v=\mathsf{Top}
with premise
\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,v,\Gamma^{\prime};s~\mathrm{prevalid}
.
We need to show
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];s[x\backslash v]\vdash\mathsf{Top}\,b[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}[x\backslash v]
.
Since
\mathsf{Top}[x\backslash v]=\mathsf{Top}
,
we need to show
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[x\backslash v];s[x\backslash v]\vdash\mathsf{Top}\,b[x\backslash v]\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}\mathsf{Top}
,
which holds by rule
Me-TAp
.

∎

Lemma 33

(Congruence of
\leq
)
.

Let
\Gamma;s
be an extended context, and let
u
,
u^{\prime}
,
v
,
t
and
\alpha
be terms
We have these six congruence relations:

If
\Gamma;v::s\vdash u\leq u^{\prime}
, then
\Gamma;s\vdash u\,v\leq u^{\prime}\,v
.

If
\Gamma;v::s\vdash u\leq^{*}u^{\prime}
, then
\Gamma;s\vdash u\,v\leq^{*}u^{\prime}\,v
.

If
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t;\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\leq u^{\prime}
then
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u\leq\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u^{\prime}
.

If
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t;\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\leq^{*}u^{\prime}
then
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u\leq^{*}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u^{\prime}
.

If
\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha;s\vdash u\leq u^{\prime}
then
\Gamma;\alpha::s\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u\leq\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u^{\prime}
.

If
\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha;s\vdash u\leq^{*}u^{\prime}
then
\Gamma;\alpha::s\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u\leq^{*}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We do the first case. Every other case is similar:
it lifts Lemmas
34
and
35
.

Suppose we have
\Gamma;v::s\vdash u\leq u^{\prime}
.
By definition of the subtype relation, we have a term
t
such that

\Gamma;v::s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t
and
\Gamma;v::s\vdash u^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t
.
By Lemma
35
, we have
\Gamma;s\vdash u\,v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t\,v

from
\Gamma;v::s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t
.
By Lemma
34
, we have
\Gamma;s\vdash u^{\prime}\,v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t\,v

from
\Gamma;v::s\vdash u^{\prime}\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}t
.
We then conclude by definition of the subtype relation with the intermediate term
t\,v
.
∎

Lemma 34

(Congruence of
\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}
)
.

Let
\Gamma;s
be an extended context, and let
u
,
u^{\prime}
,
v
and
t
be terms
We have these congruence relations:

If
\Gamma;v::s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}u^{\prime}
, then
\Gamma;s\vdash u\,v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}u^{\prime}\,v
.

If
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t;\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}u^{\prime}
then
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u^{\prime}
.

If
\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}u^{\prime}
then
\Gamma;\alpha::s\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u^{\prime}
.

Proof.

For the first case, take
\Gamma;v::s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}u^{\prime}
.
On each
\Gamma;v::s\vdash a\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b
subderivation of our initial multi-step derivation,
we apply the rule
Ms-App
, to obtain
\Gamma;s\vdash a\,v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow}}}b\,v
.
By doing so on each subderivation, we finally obtain the derivation
\Gamma;s\vdash u\,v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leq}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}u^{\prime}\,v
,
which is the desired result.

The other cases are similar, with the use of rules
Ms-Fun
and
Ms-FOp
instead.
∎

Lemma 35

(Congruence of
\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow}}}
)
.

Let
\Gamma;s
be an extended context, and let
u
,
u^{\prime}
,
v
and
t
be terms
We have these congruence relations:

If
\Gamma;v::s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}u^{\prime}
, then
\Gamma;s\vdash u\,v\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}u^{\prime}\,v
.

If
\Gamma,x\,{\leq}\,t;\mathrm{nil}\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}u^{\prime}
then
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u^{\prime}
.

If
\Gamma,x\,{\equiv}\,\alpha;s\vdash u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}u^{\prime}
then
\Gamma;\alpha::s\vdash\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u\mathop{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\equiv}}{{\longrightarrow\hskip-11.99998pt\rightarrow\hskip 1.99997pt}}}\lambda x\,{\leq}\,t.u^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Similar proof as Lemma
34
.
∎

Lemma 36

(Commutativity - context weakening)
.

Let
\Gamma;s
and
\Gamma^{\prime};s^{\prime}
be extended contexts such that

\Gamma;s\rightarrowtail\Gamma^{\prime};s^{\prime}
.

Then we have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\rightarrowtail\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}
.

Proof.

By induction on
s
.

If
s=\mathrm{nil}
:

Then the result holds.

If
s=\alpha::s_{0}
:

Then by assumption we have
\Gamma;\alpha::s_{0}\rightarrowtail\Gamma^{\prime};s^{\prime}
.
By rule
Ct-Stk
, we then have
\Gamma;s_{0}\rightarrowtail\Gamma^{\prime};s_{1}
, with
s^{\prime}=\alpha^{\prime}::s_{1}
for some term
\alpha^{\prime}
.
By induction we finally have
\Gamma;\mathrm{nil}\rightarrowtail\Gamma^{\prime};\mathrm{nil}
.

∎
